The measures proposed in Chapter 10 are therefore necessary in this connection. The Dissentients, Mr. Sørbotten and Mr. Øien, agree in essentials with the evaluation made by the Majority. However they consider that it will not be possible to maintain the building of new ships to the extent recommended by the Majority. Moreover these members consider that reduction must be effected more quickly than proposed by the Majority. The Dissentients recommend that for the time being activities should be maintained equivalent to 8000 man-hours for the building of new ships. Reduction from 20 000 to 8000 man-hours should commence in 1978 and the aim should be to achieve full reduction before the end of 1979. The Dissentients presuppose that decisions be made as soon as possible concerning the yeards that are to receive support to maintain all or part of their shipbuilding activities in the time ahead, and how many employees each yard is to have. The Dissentients recommend that in future public support for new contracts, apart from favourable supplier credits, should be given only within such an outline plan. The Dissentients further presuppose that corresponding reductions take place in other countries parallel with the reduction in Norway, and that Norwegian authorities should work to achieve this aim through international agencies To avoid excessive, harmful social and economic effects both at local levels and for the community as a whole the Committee presupposes that great effort will be made on the part of both the industry and the authorities to obtain alternative employment. The possibilities for obtaining employment for the shipyards instead of building new ships are considered in Chapter 5. As will be seen there are only limited possibilities of alternative employment for shipyards. It would appear that the offshore market is the only alternative market of any size. The re-adjustment problems industry is encounterring, indicate that it would be deserable to have a higher rate of exploration and production than is the case at present on the Norwegian continental shelf. This would be possible within the production limits stipulated by the Storting. Starting up dates and the times allowed for developing the individual fields discovered, must be determined so as to allow industry

Skipsbyggingsindustrien on land an opportunity to plan production sensibly. If there is to be an increased degree of Norwegian engagement, it is vital that development be based upon Norwegian expertise to the greatest possible extent. Therefore the authorities must work to this end. In this connection it must be borne in mind that the shipbuilding industry and other shipping industry represent the technically most advanced industry in Norway. In that capacity the shipbuilding industry is of vital importance for the whole of our industrial environment, for example through training technically and professionally highly skilled labour. Almost half of the shipyards undertake work other than shipbuilding repairs and offshore assignments. Their production is highly diversified. Reduction in the building of new ships may create excessive pressure toward performing other industrial work, which may cause difficulties for the rest of the engineering industry in Norway. Therefore it would especially be advantageous if the shipyards could use their competence to work up new markets, preferably co-operating with other Norwegian industries. Development of for example floating processing facilities and factories could lead to orders, particularly in relation to developing countries. Some shipyards could concentrate upon such fields as environmental protection equipment, lange processing facilities etc., i.e. new fields in which large sums will be invested and fields in which development may be intensified out of regard for the employment situation. A future, viable Norwegian shipyard industry will depend upon research being strengthened in the industry. Through purposeful research and development Norwegian shipyards must endeavour to create new products and better production processes with a view to creating and exploiting new markets in addition to the traditional markets. Besides the research that must be carried out within each individual enterprise, it will be necessary for this branch of industry as whole, or for groups of shipyards to carry out a number of large research projects to find new fields of work for the shipyard industry.

4.2 Recommendations. The Committee recommends that support from the authorities should concentrate upon measures to facilitate or make possible the reorganisation that must take place. The yards that have to reduce the building of new ships,

4.2 Recommendations. The Committee recommends that support from the authorities should concentrate upon measures to facilitate or make possible the reorganisation that must take place. The yards that have to reduce the building of new ships,

will have varying possibilities of expanding other activities. Some shipyards will be unable to continue the building of new ships without considerable re-organization, and in other cases to close down the entire enterprise may be the most realistic solution. In the case of enterprises that are reorganized or closed down, it may be necessary to make funds available to ensure for one thing that the employees suffer as little as possible. Judging from the investigations carried out by NSFI in collaboration with two shipyards, it appears that ship production efficiency could be improved considerably through expenditure to rationalize production. It appears reasonable that public support should take the form of financial aid in connection with capital expenditure. Aid for market analyses, product development etc. may also be necessary. Under the existing system of re-organization loans, loans are granted for up to 40% of the costs of re-organization. Such public participation by up to 40% should continue. However it is proposed that up to 20% may be given in the form of a grant, and the remainder as a loan. The Committee recommends that loans should run for a term of 12 years, with no interest or repayments for the first three years. Moreover it is proposed that a scheme be established to finance part of the cost of efficiency improvements on the same guidelines and conditions as for loans to selected shipyards for re-organization, conversion etc. In Chapter 9.3 the Committee gives an account of the shipbuilding industry's need to concentrate more upon the research and development sector in order to develop new products and better production processes. In view of the difficult position in which the shipbuilding industry is placed, the Committee considers it necessary to propose that for the year 1978 a sum of 15 million Kroner be placed at the disposal of NTNF to promote research and development activities for the shipbuilding industry. If necessary this amount should be increased during the coming 5-year period. The Committee considers that it is still necessary to provide assistance to secure new contracts for the shipyards. Assistance given by the authorities to secure new contracts for the shipyards should consist principally of improving the general credit terms, in the Committee's opinion. During an interim period the shipyards will nevertheless require direct aid to secure new contracts. It should be made a condition that shipyards that receive public aid do not employ side 46

labour hired from agencies. If for short periods the shipyard needs hired labour, it should be obtained from proper, permanently established works that are not based upon labour leasing. This must apply when work is contracted out too. Similarly it must apply in respect of Scandinavian countries/hired labour. The Committee would stress that a public system of control should be established in this respect, for example through labour employment authorities. Moreover shipyards that receive aid under established support schemes must obtain equipment etc. from Norwegian suppliers to the greatest possible extent. The Committee Majority proposes that for ships being built at Norwegian yards for Norwegian owners, it shall be possible to allow 80% delivery credit over 12 years, with no repayments for the first three years. Moreover it is proposed that the shipyards shall be allowed to offer long-term financing at 8% interest p.a., costs excluded, this conforming with OECD's export rules. Differentiation should also be considered, for example a support scheme under which loans for Norwegian contracts can be offered at 7% interest. It is proposed that the above measures should apply to contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980. The established scheme for delivery credits has served to secure new contracts in a number of cases and it is obvious that such a scheme will be needed for some time to come, as in many cases it is utterly necessary if longterm ship financing is to take place. It is proposed that a guarantee scheme be made applicable to contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980, on the same terms as under the existing guarantee scheme. The Committee considers it desirable that the ship-financing institutions do not demand unreasonable collateral and that they, possibly in collaboration with the authorities, endeavour to restrict any harmful effects the present low prices for ships may have upon the community, also in respect of contracts concluded earlier. The Dissentients largely agree with the Majority as regards the forms for support. Like the Majority they consider that to secure new contracts it will be expedient to enable the yards to offer favourable devilery credits. There is much to indicate that in future this can best be done by the Government granting interest subsidies. Moreover the Dissentients consider that the government authorities should decide the details of the terms and conditions. However terms and conditions should be reviewed from time to time in the light of developments. In the long term the goal must be to achieve ordinary commercial credit terms. The Dissentients agree with the Majority that the existing scheme as regards developing countries should be retained for the time being, on more stringent terms, but doubt whether it is realistic to expect that this scheme will bring appreciable results. It is pressupposed that any new contracts shall go to yards which the autorities consider should maintain shipbuilding activities. The building of fishing craft etc. is of considerable importance for many small and medium-sized yards. If Norwegian yards are to continue to receive a satisfactory share of this market, a favourable delivery credit scheme will be required in this sector too. The Committee is aware of the objections raised against the scheme for a 25% deduction from income for Norwegian shipowners who build ships in Norway. Although the scheme has been of considerable help in securing new contracts for Norwegian yards, the Committee is inclined to believe that the scheme should be discontinued. However it will clearly be extremely difficult to obtain new contracts. Therefore the Committee would propose that a scheme be established whereby Norwegian yards that build ships for Norwegian shipowners receive a direct grant of 10% of the contract price, it being presupposed hat such a scheme is confined to the types of ship for which the deduction from income scheme applied. It is proposed that the said scheme shall be applicable to contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980. The existing scheme of government support for exports to developing countries has been of substantial help in securing new contracts. The Committee proposes hat this scheme be maintained for contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980. Governmental measures must be applied in conformity with the presuppositions in Chapter 9 (the Majority proposal or the Dissentients' proposal). However the Majority consider that each enterprise must be allowed reasonable opportunity to prepare, in a methodical manner, the rationalization, re-organization or winding up of its own operations. This could mean that at the beginning of the re-organization periods the schemes will be of a more general character. The Dissentients consider that gradual reduction will be ensured through the contracts already concluded.

This must apply when work is contracted out too. Similarly it must apply in respect of Scandinavian countries/hired labour. Although the scheme has been of considerable help in securing new contracts for Norwegian yards, the Committee is inclined to believe that the scheme should be discontinued. It is proposed that the said scheme shall be applicable to contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980. The existing scheme of government support for exports to developing countries has been of substantial help in securing new contracts. The Committee proposes hat this scheme be maintained for contracts concluded for delivery not later than December 31 1980. Governmental measures must be applied in conformity with the presuppositions in Chapter 9 (the Majority proposal or the Dissentients' proposal). However the Majority consider that each enterprise must be allowed reasonable opportunity to prepare, in a methodical manner, the rationalization, re-organization or winding up of its own operations. This could mean that at the beginning of the re-organization periods the schemes will be of a more general character. The Dissentients consider that gradual reduction will be ensured through the contracts already concluded.

Skipsbyggingsindustrien The Committee further presupposes that current reviews are made in the light of market developments to determine the amount of support required to secure a sufficiency of new orders, and that proposals concerning any amendments necessary shall be submitted by the agency that effects the governmental measures. With the exception of certain contracts placed by public authorities and for ferries, fishing craft etc., it proved that in 1977 the average amount of support required to secure orders for Norwegian yards was about 20% of the contract price. The Committee has based its considerations upon the same amount of support being necessary in the coming years too. Further, the Committee has assumed that rationalization of the shipyards that are to continue to build ships, and conversion of the yards that are to switch to other activities, will require the investment of approximately Nkr. 100 000 per employee. It is assumed that re-organization measures for the marine equipment industry will require less, approximately Nkr. 60 000 per employee, with a number of employees that corresponds to one half of the reduction in the number of shipbuilding employees. As already mentioned, the Majority proposal presupposes that the number of employees in the shipbuilding sector will be reduced by 6000 in the course of a three-year period. It is presupposed that other employment can be found for 4000 of these 6000 in existing enterprises, through the expedient of governmental financing schemes that cover up to 40% of the re-organization costs. It is considered that the remaining 2000 will fairly quickly be absorbed by the labour market elsewhere or by natural retirement. The Majority does not consider that it will be possible to absorb a greater number in this manner. Any unemployment resulting from spiralling effects has not been included here. Support for securing contracts and for rationalization and re-organization costs has been estimated by the Majority as raughly 1930 million Kroner for the three-year period 1978-80. As previously mentioned the Dissentients presupposed reducing the number of employees in the shipbuilding sector by 12000 over a two-year period. Here it was assumed that employment for 4000 could be found within the shipbuilding industry through governmental re-organization schemes, that 4 000 of the remaining 8 000 would be absorbed by the labour market within a relatively short time, and that 4000 would

have to receive unemployment benefit. Unemployment resulting from spiralling effects is not included here. The cost of support measures for the period 1978-80 according to the Dissentients' proposal would be roughly 1270 million Kroner. These recommendations may result in substantial amounts of Government funds being placed in some enterprises. In view of this the Committee's charmain, Mr. Ulveseth, and members Knoph, Moland, Skytøen, Sørbotten, Wallestad and Øien consider it necessary to evaluate the question of appointing government representatives to sit on the boards of these enterprises. Committee members Müller and Larssen do not regard it necessary to consider this proposal. In addition the Committee chairman, Mr. Ulveseth, and members Wallestad, Skytøen and Øien submitted a proposal to the effect that the Ministry of Industry, representing the Government, should take steps to study whether there are grounds for establishing one or more joint offices for the purpose of conducting sales and contract negotiations etc. on behalf of the enterprises that participate in the joint office. The Committee would stress that in the first place the industry itself must bear the main responsibility for adapting the shipyard industry to the changed market conditions. Where governmental support measures are concerned, the Committee submits two proposals for organisation of their implementation. Committee members Anker-Nilssen, Grieg Tidemand, Larssen, Moland, Reksten, Ryver, Skytøen, Sørbotten, Wallestad and Øien propose that a select committee be established under the Ministry of Industry to steer re-organization of the shipbuilding sector in accordance with the Committee's recommendations, it being presupposed that such a committee shall have access to the necessary expertise through established institutions such as the Industrial Fund and firms of consultants with specialist knowledge. The Chairman of the Committee, Mr. Ulveseth, and members Knoph and Müller propose that the Industrial Fund shall be charged with the task of implementing the concrete plans for the necessary re-organization and re-structuring of the shipbuilding industry. A small committee of, for example, three persons should be established to work with the secretariat of the Industrial Fund, to study and decide upon implementation of the concrete reorganizing measures. In order that the best possible foundation side 48

Committee members Anker-Nilssen, Grieg Tidemand, Larssen, Moland, Reksten, Ryver, Skytøen, Sørbotten, Wallestad and Øien propose that a select committee be established under the Ministry of Industry to steer re-organization of the shipbuilding sector in accordance with the Committee's recommendations, it being presupposed that such a committee shall have access to the necessary expertise through established institutions such as the Industrial Fund and firms of consultants with specialist knowledge. The Chairman of the Committee, Mr. Ulveseth, and members Knoph and Müller propose that the Industrial Fund shall be charged with the task of implementing the concrete plans for the necessary re-organization and re-structuring of the shipbuilding industry. A small committee of, for example, three persons should be established to work with the secretariat of the Industrial Fund, to study and decide upon implementation of the concrete reorganizing measures. In order that the best possible foundation side 48

shall be obtained for determining overall solutions for the re-structuring, the Shipbuilding Committee considers that the outline conditions for the industry's future operations should be planned in cooperation between representatives from the shipyards, the trades unions and the labour employment authorities (local or county). Solutions should be sought for the future structure of each region. The Shipbuilding Comittee presupposes that the select committee mentioned above should study matters of principle relating to the restructuring with representatives from the authorities involved and with the industry's organisations, and that further work be conducted in close contact with them. The Shipbuilding Committee considers it desirable that the inter-departemental committee the «Shipping Industry Committee» consisting of representatives from the Ministries of Industry, Finance, Commerce, Municipal Affairs and Labour should be maintained. Possibly a representative from the Oil and Energy Ministry should also be appointed to this committee.
